# Signed physical-source reconstruction

The actual positive-fill signed source supports the submitted finite stationary memory interval and fitted-boundary separation. The proof below reconstructs the source, every generator contribution, the positive contraction, the moment receiver and the comparison. Finite values are retained in `attempts/001/results/`; the full-source finite qualifications visit every state and the N5000 calculation retains every magnetization sector. A fresh relocated run is the next reproducibility check. No signed-chain, negative-fill N5000, physical-seconds or asymptotic statement is inferred here.

## A01: the literal source and its clock

Let (N=5000), (sigma_i\in\{-1,1\}), and (M=\sum_i\sigma_i). The sealed parent has zero fields and only couplings (\pm1). The complete positive-fill coupling bitstream equals the complete graph with exactly the parent's 1000 negative edges. These edges form a matching. Thus 2000 sites belong to negative pairs and 3000 sites have no negative partner. The latter sites retain their dense interactions. The ordered ports are ((0,1,60,61)), negative pair ((0,1)) is observed explicitly, and ports 60 and 61 are correction-free.

With (P=1000) and ({\cal P}) the negative matching,

\[
 H(\sigma)=-\sum_{i<j}J_{ij}\sigma_i\sigma_j
 =-\frac{M^2-N}{2}+2\sum_{(i,j)\in{\cal P}}\sigma_i\sigma_j,
 \qquad \beta=1/N,\qquad
 \pi(\sigma)=Z^{-1}e^{-H(\sigma)/N}.
\]

At site (i), let (a=\sigma_i) and (b) its negative partner's sign, or (b=0) at a correction-free site. Its self-excluded local field and flip energy are

\[
 h_i=M-a-2b,\qquad H(\sigma^i)-H(\sigma)=2a h_i.
\]

There is one heatbath attempt per physical site per unit time. A unit attempt redraws the site from its Gibbs conditional law, so the actual flip rate is

\[
 R(M,a,b)=\frac1{1+\exp\{2a(M-a-2b)/N\}},\qquad
 (Lu)(\sigma)=\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}R_i(\sigma)[u(\sigma^i)-u(\sigma)].
\]

No factor (1/N) multiplies this generator. The inverse temperature already scales the complete Hamiltonian. Every physical site, including the free sites, participates in the sum. The ratio (R_i(\sigma)/R_i(\sigma^i)=e^{-[H(\sigma^i)-H(\sigma)]/N}) gives detailed balance. All flip rates are positive, making the finite chain irreducible. Global sign reversal preserves both Gibbs law and generator.

Write (A=-L), (f(\sigma)=\sigma_0=s), and (t=\sigma_1). Then

\[
 g(M,s,t):=(Af)(\sigma)=2sR(M,s,t).
\]

For a general observable, each heatbath component is (A_i=I-E_i), with (E_i) the conditional-expectation orthogonal projector in (L^2(\pi)). Hence (0\le A_i\le I), (0\le A\le NI), (A) is self-adjoint, (\langle f,1\rangle_\pi=0), and (\|f\|_\pi=1). Only (A_0f) is nonzero. Thus

\[
 \mu_0=1,\quad \mu_1=\langle f,g\rangle_\pi,\quad
 \mu_2=\langle g,g\rangle_\pi=\mu_1,
 \quad \mu_3=\langle g,Ag\rangle_\pi,\quad
 \mu_4=\langle Ag,Ag\rangle_\pi.
\]

The identity (\mu_2=\mu_1) follows from (A_0^2=A_0); the implementation computes both expectations before checking their overlap. It is a clock-specific identity: multiplying all attempts by (\alpha) instead gives (\mu_2=\alpha\mu_1).

### Every term of the physical second action

At fixed (M,s,t), marked flips contribute

\[
 D=R(M,s,t)[g(M,s,t)-g(M-2s,-s,t)]
   +R(M,t,s)[g(M,s,t)-g(M-2t,s,-t)].
\]

For (a\in\{-1,1\}), put (d_a=g(M,s,t)-g(M-2a,s,t)), and (F_a=R(M,a,0)d_a). For an unmarked negative pair in the oriented state ((a,b)), put

\[
 C_{ab}=R(M,a,b)d_a+R(M,b,a)d_b,\qquad
 \ell_{ab}=(a+b+2)/2.
\]

There are (K=P-1=999) unmarked negative pairs and (F=N-2P=3000) free sites. If (k) counts plus spins outside the marked pair, (n_{ab}) counts each of the four oriented pair states, and (k_f=k-\sum\ell_{ab}n_{ab}), then the sum of **all** physical flips is

\[
 Ag=D+(F-k_f)F_{-1}+k_fF_{+1}+\sum_{ab}n_{ab}C_{ab}
   =B_0+\sum_{ab}B_{ab}n_{ab},
\]

where

\[
 B_0=D+F F_{-1}+k(F_{+1}-F_{-1}),\qquad
 B_{ab}=C_{ab}-\ell_{ab}(F_{+1}-F_{-1}).
\]

The code uses these unambiguous indexed expressions. Both marked flips, both spins of every unmarked pair, and every free spin appear. This is an additive static representation of (A^2f), rather than a claim that the microscopic trajectories are lumpable by magnetization or these counts.

## A02: positive factors, multiplicities and absolute partition

Let (O(\sigma)) be the number of opposed negative pairs. Then (\sum_{{\cal P}}\sigma_i\sigma_j=P-2O), so

\[
 e^{-H/N}=e^{-1/2-2P/N}\,e^{M^2/(2N)}r^{O},\qquad r=e^{4/N}.
\]

The absolute prefactor is necessary even though it cancels in normalized moments. One negative pair has the four oriented weights (1,r,r,1) and generating polynomial

\[
 p(x)=1+2rx+x^2.
\]

A free site contributes (1+x). The marked pair contributes (r^{1_{s\ne t}}). Consequently the bulk partition coefficients are

\[
 P_j(k)=[x^k]p(x)^{K-j}(1+x)^F,\qquad j=0,1,2.
\]

Invalid degrees or negative remaining pair counts have coefficient zero. For (q=(a,b)), let (u_q=r^{1_{a\ne b}}) and (\ell_q=\ell_{ab}). Counting an ordered marked unobserved pair yields the unnormalized conditional sums

\[
 N_q(k)=K u_q P_1(k-\ell_q),
\]
\[
 N_{qr}(k)=K(K-1)u_qu_r P_2(k-\ell_q-\ell_r).
\]

Here (N_{qr}) represents (n_q(n_r-1_{q=r})). The four oriented categories retain the two opposed states separately. Combining them would give the factor 2 in (p(x)). The exact square includes all 16 ordered second-factorial terms:

\[
 \sum (Ag)W=B_0P_0+\sum_qB_qN_q,
\]
\[
 \sum (Ag)^2W=B_0^2P_0+2B_0\sum_qB_qN_q
       +\sum_qB_q^2N_q+\sum_{q,r}B_qB_rN_{qr}.
\]

For each marked orientation and each (0\le k\le N-2), multiply these quantities by

\[
 r^{1_{s\ne t}}\exp\{M^2/(2N)\},\qquad M=2k+s+t-(N-2).
\]

The five corresponding numerator summands are (P_0,sgP_0,g^2P_0,g\sum AgW,\sum(Ag)^2W). Divide by the common zeroth sum. Multiply that sum by (e^{-1/2-2P/N}) for the absolute partition. This derives every moment used by A03 directly from the complete source.

### Positive insertion series and complete omitted tail

Set (c=2(r-1)>0). For (n=2K+F),

\[
 p(x)^K(1+x)^F
 =\sum_{j=0}^{K}{K\choose j}c^j x^j(1+x)^{n-2j},
\]

so a coefficient is a sum of nonnegative terms

\[
 T_j={K\choose j}c^j{n-2j\choose k-j}.
\]

Whenever the next term is nonzero,

\[
 \frac{T_{j+1}}{T_j}=
 \frac{(K-j)c}{j+1}
 \frac{(k-j)(n-k-j)}{(n-2j)(n-2j-1)}
 \le\frac{Kc/2}{j+1}.
\]

For the last inequality, put (a=k-j,b=n-k-j,m=a+b\ge2). Then (ab\le m^2/4\le m(m-1)/2). If (\eta=Kc/2) and (\eta/(J+2)<1), truncating after (J) pays

\[
 0\le\sum_{j>J}T_j
 \le {n\choose k}\frac{\eta^{J+1}}{(J+1)!}
             \frac1{1-\eta/(J+2)}.
\]

When the expansion terminates by (J), this tail is exactly zero. The runner uses (J=48), exact integer binomial rows, and directed Arb arithmetic. It adds the complete one-sided coefficient tail before any signed moment combination. Cancellation in (Ag) and its square is handled by interval arithmetic. The numerical tail bounds, as well as all arithmetic endpoint widths, are retained; no finite-sector or pair-count truncation is hidden.

### Port and normalization interfaces

Retaining ports ((0,1,60,61)) removes one negative pair and two free sites. For a mask (b\in\{0,\ldots,15\}), with (r_b=\operatorname{popcount}(b)), its absolute Gibbs marginal is

\[
 W_b=e^{-1/2-2P/N}r^{1_{b_0\ne b_1}}
 \sum_{k=0}^{N-4}[x^k]p(x)^{P-1}(1+x)^{F-2}
       \exp\{[2(k+r_b)-N]^2/(2N)\}.
\]

Every normalized ratio (W_b/W_0), the absolute value (\log W_0), and (\sum_bW_b=Z) are reconstructed. Separately,

\[
 Z=e^{-1/2-2P/N}\sum_{k=0}^{N}[x^k]p(x)^P(1+x)^F
                    e^{(2k-N)^2/(2N)}.
\]

All factors are palindromic, so folding this even-(N) sum gives multiplicity 2 for (k<N/2) and exactly 1 for the central row (k=N/2). Both full and folded sums are computed. The coefficient sum also matches (p(1)^K2^F=(2+2r)^K2^F), checking all component orientation counts independently of the magnetization Gibbs factor.

The finite N15 root and N20 root-plus-tail tests use the literal induced original vertices, the same positive fill, beta (1/N), and the same unit physical-site heatbath convention. Exact Gray-code fields and energy updates visit every one of their (2^N) states. Integer signatures coalesce identical expectation summands only after exact state visitation. This is an exact full-state contraction, with neither an assumed Markov quotient nor a floating source fit. The count adapter and full-state calculation agree in all five moments and absolute partition. These finite roots qualify the implementation; they do not replace the N5000 source.

## A03: proved finite moment receiver and information payment

The spectral theorem applied to (A) and (f) gives a positive probability measure (\nu) on ([0,N]), with

\[
 \mu_j=\int\lambda^j\,d\nu(\lambda),\qquad
 c(t)=\langle f,e^{-tA}f\rangle_\pi
     =\int e^{-t\lambda}\,d\nu(\lambda).
\]

The constant eigenfunction has zero (f)-component; no full-source gap value is assumed. The moment matrices are the Gram matrices of (1,\lambda) in (L^2(\nu)) and (L^2(\lambda\nu)). Their determinants are respectively (\mu_0\mu_2-\mu_1^2) and (\mu_1\mu_3-\mu_2^2). The fresh directed evaluation makes both strictly positive. Thus both two-node rules below are well-defined for this source.

For any positive measure with moments (m_0,m_1,m_2,m_3) and (d=m_0m_2-m_1^2>0), its monic degree-two orthogonal polynomial is

\[
 P_2(x)=x^2+ax+b,\qquad
 a=\frac{m_1m_2-m_0m_3}{d},\qquad
 b=-\frac{m_2+am_1}{m_0}.
\]

Its roots (x_-<x_+) lie in the convex hull of the measure's support and its Gaussian weights are positive:

\[
 w_-=(m_0x_+-m_1)/(x_+-x_-),\qquad
 w_+=(m_1-m_0x_-)/(x_+-x_-).
\]

For completeness, the compressed multiplication operator on the span of (1,x), in the positive Gram metric, is self-adjoint and has these two eigenvalues. The Rayleigh quotient puts them in the support interval. The spectral weights of the constant vector are nonnegative; a zero weight would force the minimal polynomial degree below two and contradict (d>0). If a two-atom measure gives exact closure, the same rule is the exact integral. Otherwise the standard orthogonality sign-change argument gives two distinct interior nodes.

The rule matches degrees zero through three. To see this directly, divide a cubic by (P_2). The residual has degree at most one and is integrated exactly by the weight equations, while (P_2) and (xP_2) have zero true integral and vanish at both nodes.

Let (h) have nonnegative fourth derivative on the support. Its Hermite interpolating cubic at the two nodes, with both values and first derivatives matched, obeys

\[
 h(x)-H_3(x)=\frac{h^{(4)}(\xi_x)}{4!}(x-x_-)^2(x-x_+)^2\ge0.
\]

Integrating and using cubic exactness gives the Gaussian lower inequality. It applies to (h(x)=e^{-tx}), (t\ge0). Applying it instead to the positive measure (\lambda\nu) and

\[
 h_t(x)=\int_0^t e^{-ux}\,du=(1-e^{-tx})/x,\qquad h_t(0)=t,
\]

also works because (h_t^{(4)}(x)=\int_0^t u^4e^{-ux}\,du\ge0). Hence

\[
 L_2(t)=\sum_iw_i e^{-tx_i}\le c(t),\qquad
 c(t)\le U_2(t)=1-\sum_i\widetilde w_i h_t(\widetilde x_i),
\]

where the first rule uses ((\mu_0,\ldots,\mu_3)), and the tilded rule uses ((\mu_1,\ldots,\mu_4)). This is the left-zero Radau upper sign, including its normalization. The actual directed nodes are strictly positive; the runner evaluates these explicit scalar formulas rather than the earlier matrix-compression code. No uncertain pivot is deflated. For a degenerate measure, exact finite-support closure must be resolved instead; a zero node uses the continuous definition (h_t(0)=t). Those possibilities do not arise in the strictly positive determinants and nodes established here.

At (t=1/3,2/3), the arithmetic balls for (L_2) and (U_2) are replaced by the exact rational-endpoint hull ([\inf L_2,\sup U_2]). This pays the quadrature approximation, rather than mistaking the narrow endpoint-arithmetic balls for exact physical correlations.

### Binary law and the complete reference

For stationary readings (S_0,S_t,S_{2t}\in\{-1,1\}), inversion symmetry removes the odd Fourier characters. Put (x=c(t),z=c(2t)). Then

\[
 p(a,b,c)=\tfrac18[1+xab+xbc+zac].
\]

The exact adjacent-pair-matched Markov reference is

\[
 q(a,b,c)=\tfrac18(1+xab)(1+xbc).
\]

Writing (u=ab,v=bc), each increment cell has two middle-spin copies, giving

\[
 p_{uv}=\tfrac14[1+x(u+v)+zuv],\qquad
 q_{uv}=\tfrac14(1+xu)(1+xv),\qquad
 I(S_0;S_{2t}\mid S_t)=\sum_{u,v}p_{uv}\log(p_{uv}/q_{uv}).
\]

This keeps every reference cell and uses natural logarithms. With (\Delta=z-x^2), (p-q=\Delta uv/4). The reversible measure ensures (\Delta\ge0) by covariance of the two decreasing exponential functions. The fresh correlation hulls give a strictly positive defect and strictly positive physical/reference cells.

Set (r_{uv}=\Delta uv/(4q_{uv})). After exact cancellation of the normalization-linear term,

\[
 I=\sum_{uv}q_{uv}\left[(1+r_{uv})\log(1+r_{uv})-r_{uv}\right]
  =\sum_{uv}q_{uv}\sum_{k=2}^{16}\frac{(-1)^kr_{uv}^k}{k(k-1)}+R.
\]

The directed check establishes (|r_{uv}|<1). Therefore the entire omitted series satisfies

\[
 |R|\le\sum_{uv}
 \frac{q_{uv}|r_{uv}|^{17}}{16\cdot17(1-|r_{uv}|)}.
\]

The runner adds this symmetric absolute payment after outward interval evaluation of every series term. It separately evaluates the direct four-cell KL as a diagnostic and checks overlap. The source insertion tails, directed rounding, quadrature gaps, correlation hulls, complete reference denominator uncertainty and series tail have therefore all been paid. A lower endpoint from this calculation applies to the actual microscopic information without a truncated dynamical semigroup assumption.

## A04: same-boundary clock and strict comparison

The fresh (W_b) determine (\pi_b=W_b/\sum W), and the declared four-port column generator is

\[
 Q_{b\mathbin{\mathrm{xor}}2^i,b}=\frac{W_{b\mathbin{\mathrm{xor}}2^i}}{W_b+W_{b\mathbin{\mathrm{xor}}2^i}},\quad i=0,1,2,3,
 \qquad Q_{bb}=-\sum_{c\ne b}Q_{cb}.
\]

This is the stated boundary kinetic completion. It is reversible and irreducible with stationary law (\pi). The retained visible spin is (f_b=+1) when bit 0 is set and (-1) otherwise. Its stationary correlation at scalar speed (\alpha\ge0) is

\[
 c_B(\alpha,t)=f^T e^{\alpha tQ}\operatorname{diag}(\pi)f.
\]

The full stationary signed vector (\pi f) enters; there is no conditional reset. With (A_B=-Q^T), its centered positive spectral measure has support in ((0,4]). Thus for (t>0),

\[
 \frac{\partial c_B}{\partial\alpha}
 =-t\int\lambda e^{-\alpha t\lambda}\,d\nu_B(\lambda)<0,
 \qquad c_B(0,t)=1,\quad \lim_{\alpha\to\infty}c_B(\alpha,t)=0.
\]

Consequently each exact adjacent microscopic correlation in ((0,1)) has exactly one nonnegative scalar fit. Since that correlation lies in the paid microscopic hull, bisections against its two rational endpoints produce ([\alpha_-,\alpha_+]) containing **every** matching scalar clock. Directed endpoint signs are retained. Monotonicity bounds (c_B(\alpha,t)) and (c_B(\alpha,2t)) by the endpoint evaluations; this avoids the extra interval dependency from exponentiating a whole speed interval at once.

For any inversion-symmetric binary triple with adjacent correlations (x,y), the elementary inequality (\log r\le r-1) gives

\[
 I\le\chi^2(p,q)=\frac{[z-xy]^2}{(1-x^2)(1-y^2)}.
\]

Apply this with the boundary equal-wait correlation hulls and the strictly positive reference denominators. The upper endpoint bounds the boundary CMI for every fitting scalar clock. Dividing the actual microscopic information lower endpoint by this boundary upper proves the strict reported ratio. It compares exactly this boundary generator's scalar rescalings; arbitrary kinetics or a physical-seconds map are outside the statement.

## Hypothesis-breaking controls SA01–SA03

**SA01.** At unchanged complete Gibbs prefactor, replace (r=e^{4/N}) by (e^{-4/N}), or by (e^{2/N}). Direct complete small polynomial multiplication then gives an absolute partition disjoint from literal full-state Gibbs enumeration, on both N15 and N20. Normalizing either wrong result would discard the discrepancy; the test explicitly retains absolute normalization.

**SA02.** In the literal full-state action, remove only the marked negative partner's flip term from (Ag); separately retain only the observed site's flip term and remove every nonlocal contribution. Compute the resulting third/fourth moments under the unchanged true Gibbs law. Each changed fourth moment is disjoint from the correct one on both finite sources. This detects a physical-action omission without changing stationary preparation.

**SA03.** On actual N5000, compare configurations with plus set ({1}) and ({2}), all other sites minus. Both have visible sign minus and (M=-N+2), but the negative partner differs. The visible flip-rate exponents are (2(N-1)/N) and (2(N-5)/N), giving unequal exit rates into the other visible class. Thus the visible/count partition fails strong lumpability. For the four-port projection, compare all-minus with plus set ({2}); all four ports remain minus, while the visible exponents are (2(N-3)/N) and (2(N-5)/N), again unequal. These explicit source witnesses forbid either projection from being silently substituted for microscopic dynamics.

Even after adjacent-correlation fitting, a scalar boundary clock does not inherit the physical generator moments. The unit boundary heatbath identity is (mu_{2,B}=mu_{1,B}), while its scalar rescaling gives (mu_{2,\alpha}=alpha\mu_{1,\alpha}). The actual unit microscopic identity is (mu_2=mu_1). The retained fitted first and second boundary moments are both disjoint from their microscopic counterparts. The comparison in A04 remains meaningful because it is an explicitly different generator, whose finite information has been bounded separately.

The next step is a fresh public-only replay and review of the retained endpoint and source hashes. No new source calculation is needed to state the present four claims.

# Hypotheses and scope map

All four claims hold under the explicit source, clock and reference hypotheses below. The wrong controls expose concrete failures of those hypotheses; no uncertainty is assigned to an uncomputed source. The next step is the case lead's public replay/review.

| Item | Necessary hypotheses and retained operands | What breaks or changes it |
|---|---|---|
| A01 | Original complete signed_packet N5000 graph, positive dense fill, zero fields, 1000 disjoint negative pairs, 3000 free sites, ports [0,1,60,61], observed site 0/partner 1, whole-scaled beta 1/N, stationary Gibbs law, unit attempted heatbath per physical site. | Nonzero fields change inversion symmetry; changed couplings, fixed sparse strength, negative fill, total-system attempts, accepted-mass preparation, or removing any physical flip changes the calculation. |
| A02 | Same law; every oriented component state; every magnetization sector; marked pair retained; exact first/second factorial insertions; positive expansion c=2(exp(4/N)-1)>0; certified tail condition eta/(J+2)<1. | Wrong pair sign/exponent, lost opposed-state orientation factor, omitted factorial diagonals/cross terms, removed absolute Gibbs prefactor, or doubled folded center invalidates the relevant identity. |
| A03 | Actual source moments mu0..mu4, reversibility and positivity, centered norm-one visible spin, positive source and lambda-weighted Hankel determinants, directed scalar quadrature, physical endpoint hulls, stationary inversion-symmetric three-time law, complete Markov reference, positive cell margins, paid KL series tail. | An incompatible moment sequence, uncertain deflated pivot, reversed Gauss/Radau sign, endpoint-only arithmetic without quadrature gap, changed preparation, omitted odd Fourier terms under a field, or unpaid KL tail is not this certificate. |
| A04 | Original four-port marginal rebuilt independently, declared unit bit-attempt boundary heatbath, full stationary signed preparation, nonnegative common scalar multiplier, same waits and observed bit, strict spectral monotonicity, directed fitting endpoint signs and paid chi-square denominators. | An arbitrary boundary generator or separate edge/hidden kinetic fit is outside the scalar comparison. It cannot infer microscopic dynamics or seconds from marginal Gibbs data. |
| SA01 | Correct full finite-root Gibbs law stays fixed while only the factor is intentionally corrupted. | Normalizing away the absolute discrepancy would weaken the test; absolute partitions are retained. |
| SA02 | Correct finite Gibbs law stays fixed while marked-partner or all nonlocal A^2f terms are intentionally removed. | Changing the Gibbs law simultaneously would obscure the kinetic omission; this is not done. |
| SA03 | Literal actual source states share the proposed quotient label or port mask. | Unequal visible-class exit rates refute strong lumpability. Adjacent-correlation calibration alone supplies no microscopic semigroup identity. |

The N15 and N20 qualifications are finite induced original vertex sets with positive dense fill and beta 1/15 or 1/20. They are not the original N5000 system, a generic installed triangle example, or a negative-fill source. Their role is exhaustive implementation qualification under the same source grammar and heatbath law.

The parent compressed-file SHA-256 and the parent's logical source hash are different objects and are kept separately in INPUT_SEAL.json and SOURCE.json. The dense coupling payload is decompressed and compared byte for byte with the actual parent plus positive fill. The former is a file hash; the latter names the historical logical source. The earlier marginal weights and submitted moment/certificate numbers are comparison operands, never the input used to generate the fresh source moments or boundary law.

Analytic evidence consists of the complete source/generator/count identities, the positive tail bound, the spectral and quadrature inequalities, the exact binary KL law, and monotonicity covering every fitting scalar clock. Exact-host evidence consists of integer graph/bitstream matching, every finite-state visitation, Gray-code energy identities and DOS multiplicities. Directed-host evidence consists of Arb source factors, Gibbs expectations, endpoint signs, quadrature and KL bounds, and 16-state boundary exponentials. Direct KL subtraction is a diagnostic cross-check. Earlier native/source reports are inherited assertions and definitions; no installed research engine or earlier script is executed.

The relocated run is an internal self-replay of the separate implementation. It is not a different-model, external-human or outside R3 replay. The track assigns no I2, I3 or R3 label and no director approval. No signed-chain or negative-fill N5000 CMI, all-N signed amplitude, continuum theorem, accepted-mass memory, source-equilibration cost, physical seconds, or capacity statement follows.

The all-time source estimate and growing-wait theorem are established by the complete written argument in `DERIVATION.md`. It checks all 12,497,500 stored positive pair bits, all 2501 folded rows including the central row once, an independent 5001-row count contraction, and a direct 64-state sparse N6 qualification. No large joint-density archive or research engine is used.

Every displayed number below comes from `REPORT_DATA.json`, derived from `attempts/03/RESULTS.json` and the execution/replay receipts. Exact rational outward endpoints remain in those files.

At wait 4, the lower information ratio is above 22.08. With cycle cost 2t+p and identical independent stationary preparation/readout cost p≥0, the lower information-per-cycle-time ratio is **>1.8403125402**, hence above 1.84. | Item | Outcome | Evidence |

| B01 | HELD | All-time original-source Dirichlet and forcing estimates; explicit uniform time remainder. |
| B02 | HELD | Uniform expansion for fixed t0>0 and t<=T_N=o(sqrtN), with lower-cell margin and both error terms; saturated coefficient only in joint divergent/sub-square-root limit. |

The uniform information expansion is

           +O((1+t)N^-3/2+(1+t)²N^-2),

for fixed t0>0 and t0≤t≤T_N=o(sqrtN), with an explicit uniform positive reference-cell margin. Saturation to N I_N→c_*²/2 additionally requires t_N→∞. Bounded waits retain the tanh factor. The registered square-root/longer-time controls reject an unsupported limit inference; they do not assert a counterexample to the actual source dynamics at those times.

These are separate leading objectives. Equal waits at fixed total time maximize the reversible Markov defect, which alone does not maximize CMI. The stationary one-pair Gibbs term and its degenerate equality cases remain explicit in the derivation.

# Independent derivation for B01–B04 and GB01–GB03

This reconstruction uses the complete uniform critical source, the original
physical-site attempt clock, stationary Gibbs preparation, one labelled spin,
and natural logarithms. It reconstructs the all-time estimate and the growing
wait result analytically, and supplies separately written directed scalar and
sparse checks for the finite statements. Earlier proofs are definition and
comparison inputs; the argument and implementation here are separate.

## 1. Exact source, normalization and clock

There are N spins s_i∈{-1,1}, M=Σ_i s_i and m=M/N. All unordered pair
couplings are +1, every field is zero, and

    H(s)=−Σ_{i<j}s_i s_j=−(M²−N)/2,
    π_N(s)=Z_N^−1 exp[(M²−N)/(2N)].                  (1)

Thus β=1/N. The copied public source specifies N=5000, the ordered ports
[0,1,60,61], and the complete bit array. The runner verifies every stored
coupling bit, the raw hash, all fields and unused padding. Its positive bit
count equals N(N−1)/2, which makes (1) exact for this source. Only the labelled
site zero is observed in this track. No labelled hidden-port mask is replaced
by a magnetization count, and no signed source is assigned this source law.

At rate one for **each physical site**, replace that site's spin by its Gibbs
conditional draw. Writing E_i for that conditional expectation operator,

    L=Σ_i(E_i−I),
    Ls_i=−s_i+tanh(m−s_i/N),
    r_i(s)=[1−s_i tanh((M−s_i)/N)]/2.               (2)

The second expression r_i is the actual flip rate. Attempted updates which
retain the spin still consume the declared clock. Dividing L by N would give
a different, total-system attempt clock. All results below use (2).

The count k of positive spins has unnormalized stationary weight

    C(N,k) exp[((2k−N)²−N)/(2N)].                   (3)

For even N, pair k with N−k, counting the central k=N/2 only once. Let
k=N/2+j, j=0,…,N/2; the folded multiplicity is 1 at j=0 and 2 otherwise.
Define a_0=1 and

    a_{j+1}/a_j = (N−k)/(k+1) exp[2(2j+1)/N].      (4)

Then a_j is the one-row weight divided by the central one-row weight.
The physical partition function is exactly

    Z_N=C(N,N/2)e^−1/2 Σ_j multiplicity_j a_j.       (5)

Equations (3) and (4) are two independent contraction routes used by the new
runner. It sums every one of the 2501 folded rows by (4), and independently
sums all 5001 count rows by (3). It checks the absolute partition overlap and
second/fourth/sixth moment overlaps. The retained folded table includes every
binomial count, energy, Gibbs exponent, multiplicity, relative weight and
normalized row probability. There is no source cutoff or omitted source tail.

The count identity

    C(N,N/2)+2Σ_{j=1}^{N/2}C(N,N/2+j)=2^N           (6)

also detects a missing or doubled central row exactly: its error is respectively
−C(N,N/2) or +C(N,N/2). Normalizing a malformed source cannot repair (6).
If desired, the normalized even moments change too, since the central row
adds partition mass but has zero second/fourth/sixth numerator.

## 2. Critical moments with an error bound uniform in N

This section reconstructs the static lemma instead of treating it as a fitted
scaling law. Completing the Gaussian square gives

    exp(M²/(2N))=sqrt(N/(2π))∫exp(−Ny²/2+yM)dy.

After summing the spins, the exact auxiliary field Y has density proportional
to exp[Nφ(y)], where φ(y)=log cosh y−y²/2. Conditional on Y=y, the N spins
are independent and have mean tanh y. This is an exact representation of (1),
including the normalization factor e^−1/2 2^N sqrt(N/(2π)); it does not replace
the dynamics by an auxiliary-field process.

Choose fixed sufficiently small δ>0. Taylor's theorem and continuity give
finite constants C_6,c_0>0 such that on |y|≤δ,

    |φ(y)+y⁴/12|≤C_6|y|⁶,    φ(y)≤−c_0y⁴.        (7)

These constants can be chosen as the maximum of the continuous sixth-remainder
ratio and the minimum of −φ(y)/y⁴, with its continuous value 1/12 at zero.
Indeed φ'(y)=tanh y−y<0 for y>0. On δ≤|y|≤4, continuity gives φ≤−η<0;
on |y|≥4, log cosh y≤|y| implies φ≤−y²/4.

Put U=N^1/4Y. Both exponents in the inner range are at most −c u⁴, with
c=min(c_0,1/12). The mean-value inequality for exponentials therefore gives

    |exp[Nφ(uN^−1/4)]−exp(−u⁴/12)|
      ≤C_6 N^−1/2 |u|⁶ exp(−c u⁴).                (8)

For every fixed polynomial weight |u|^p this majorant is integrable. The middle
range contributes a polynomial in N times e^−ηN. The outer range contributes
a Gaussian tail starting at |u|=4N^1/4, also exponentially small compared with
N^−1/2. The omitted quartic comparison tail is exponentially small as well.
Consequently each fixed weighted integral differs from its quartic integral
by O_p(N^−1/2), with a constant independent of N. The denominator tends to
the strictly positive quartic integral, so division preserves that error.
Finitely many smaller N can be absorbed into the same constant. In particular,

    E U²=c_*+O(N^−1/2),   E|U|^(2j)=O_j(1),
    c_*=sqrt(12)Γ(3/4)/Γ(1/4).                     (9)

The gamma ratio follows directly by z=u⁴/12 in the two even integrals.

Let a=tanh Y and Z=m−a. Conditional independence gives exactly

    E[m²|Y]=a²+(1−a²)/N.                          (10)

Since tanh²y=y²+O(y⁴) locally and the tails above are controlled,
E a²=c_*/sqrt(N)+O(N^−1). For a fixed positive integer j, expand the
conditional centered average Z in 2j powers. A term with a singleton index
has expectation zero. Every remaining term has at most j distinct indices,
there are O_j(N^j) such terms, and each factor is bounded by two. Hence

    E[|Z|^(2j)|Y]≤C_j N^−j,
    E|m|^(2j)≤2^(2j−1)(E|a|^(2j)+C_jN^−j).

Together with (9) this establishes, with constants independent of N,

    μ2:=E m²=c_*/sqrt(N)+O(N^−1),
    μ4:=E m⁴=O(N^−1),   μ6:=E m⁶=O(N^−3/2).       (11)

The argument establishes all the moments and tails used below, rather than
interchanging a weak limit with an unbounded polynomial. Numerical all-N
values of these constants have not been claimed or fitted.

## 3. Exact all-time Dirichlet estimate and B01

In L²(π_N), each E_i is an orthogonal projection. Reversibility follows from
conditional Gibbs resampling. Thus A=−L is nonnegative self-adjoint and

    <m,Am>=Σ_i E Var(m|all spins except i)
           =N^−2Σ_i E Var(s_i|all spins except i)≤1/N.  (12)

The finite-dimensional spectral theorem gives a positive measure for m with
mass μ2. Since 0≤1−e^−tλ≤tλ for λ≥0,

    0≤μ2−E[m_t m_0]≤t/N,
    E[(m_t−m_0)²]≤2t/N,   for every t≥0.          (13)

No constant in (13) depends on a fixed observation horizon.

The full stationary path law of (1)–(2) is permutation invariant. For any
bounded permutation-invariant path functional F, all E[F s_i(0)] are equal;
averaging over i yields E[F s_i(0)]=E[F m_0]. In particular,

    E[tanh(m_t)s_0(0)]=E[tanh(m_t)m_0].            (14)

Let c_N(t)=E[s_0(t)s_0(0)]. Differentiating the finite semigroup and using
(2), the 1-Lipschitz property of tanh pays at most 1/N for replacing
m_t−s_0(t)/N by m_t. Also, for every real x,

    |tanh x−x|≤|x|³/3;                            (15)

for x≥0 integrate 1−sech²x=tanh²x≤x², then use oddness. Stationarity,
(13), (14), and Cauchy–Schwarz now give

    c_N'(t)=−c_N(t)+μ2+e_N(t),
    |e_N(t)|≤(1+t)/N+sqrt(μ6 μ2)/3,  t≥0.         (16)

The three payments are respectively self-exclusion, magnetization correlation
loss, and the cubic nonlinearity under the original stationary moments.
Let B_N=sqrt(μ6 μ2)/3. Variation of constants from c_N(0)=1 gives

    c_N(t)=e^−t+(1−e^−t)μ2+R_N(t),
    |R_N(t)|≤[t−1+e^−t]/N+(1−e^−t)(1/N+B_N)
             =t/N+(1−e^−t)B_N≤C(1+t)/N.          (17)

The last constant is independent of time and N by (11). The estimate itself
holds at every t≥0; for sufficiently large t it is a broad valid bound, not
a claim of a nonzero asymptotic correlation plateau at fixed N. In a finite
irreducible source c_N(t) actually tends to zero. This proves B01 in its
original clock and source scope.

## 4. Complete information law, positive cells, and B02

For stationary readings (S_0,S_t,S_2t), global spin inversion kills every odd
Fourier moment. The binary-cube Fourier identity is therefore exactly

    p(a,b,c)=[1+xab+xbc+zac]/8,
    x=c_N(t), z=c_N(2t).                          (18)

The adjacent-pair-matched Markov law is
q(a,b,c)=(1+xab)(1+xbc)/8. Set u=ab, v=bc; the two choices of middle spin
have equal probabilities, so the KL and the conditional mutual information
are the four-cell quantities

    P_uv=[1+x(u+v)+zuv]/4,
    Q_uv=(1+xu)(1+xv)/4,
    D=z−x²,    P_uv−Q_uv=Duv/4.                   (19)

These are stationary unconditioned readings; conditional preparation or
accepted-mass histories are different objects.

Write q=e^−t and a=μ2+(1−μ2)q. Direct algebra gives

    a(2t)−a(t)²=μ2(1−μ2)(1−q)².                 (20)

Using (17) at t and 2t, the extra terms are
R_N(2t)−2aR_N(t)−R_N(t)². Since 0≤a≤1, equations (11), (17), and (20)
give, uniformly on the declared range,

    D=c_*(1−q)²/sqrt(N)+O((1+t)/N).              (21)

Here and below t_0>0 is fixed, and t_0≤t≤T_N with T_N=o(sqrt(N)). For all
large N this lies in t≤sqrt(N). Equations (11), (17) imply uniformly
x=q+O(N^−1/2), because t/N≤N^−1/2; (21) similarly gives D=O(N^−1/2).
The constants in these statements can be fixed independently of N,T_N,t;
the sufficiently-large-N threshold may depend on the chosen range.

Let q_0=e^−t_0<1. Eventually x≤(1+q_0)/2 and x≥0 (the latter also follows
from the positive spectral representation of s_0). Thus

    min_uv Q_uv=(1−x)²/4≥(1−q_0)²/16=:κ>0.        (22)

Eventually |D|≤2κ, so |D|/(4Q_uv)≤1/2 and every interpolated cell
Q_uv+rDuv/4, 0≤r≤1, is at least κ/2. This supplies the lower-cell
condition uniformly; it is not inferred merely from the words positive wait.

For fixed x define F(D)=ΣP_uv log(P_uv/Q_uv). At zero, F=F'=0;
F''(0)=Σ(uv/4)²/Q_uv=1/(1−x²)². The third derivative obeys

    |F'''(D)|≤[64(1−θ)²]^−1ΣQ_uv^−2,
    θ=max_uv |D|/(4Q_uv).                         (23)

Taylor's theorem gives the paid bound

    |I−D²/[2(1−x²)²]|
      ≤|D|³ ΣQ_uv^−2/[384(1−θ)²].                (24)

Under (22), this is uniformly O(N^−3/2). Squaring (21) pays
O((1+t)N^−3/2+(1+t)²N^−2). The uniformly smooth reciprocal denominator
changes by O(N^−1/2) when x is replaced by q; multiplying by D²=O(N^−1)
pays only O(N^−3/2). Finally
(1−q)^4/(1−q²)²=(1−q)²/(1+q)²=tanh²(t/2), so

    I_micro,N(t)=c_*² tanh²(t/2)/(2N)
      +O((1+t)N^−3/2+(1+t)²N^−2),              (25)

uniformly on the stated range. The constants depend on t_0 and the static
bounds, not on an unstated fixed-T Gronwall constant.

If t_N→∞ and t_N=o(sqrt(N)), multiplication by N makes both errors tend
to zero, and tanh²(t_N/2)→1. Therefore

    N I_micro,N(t_N)→c_*²/2.                     (26)

For fixed a>0, t_N=a log N satisfies both hypotheses. If instead t_N=t>0
is fixed, the coefficient is c_*² tanh²(t/2)/2. In particular, replacing it
by c_*²/2 at t=1/3 loses the strictly smaller factor tanh²(1/6). This is
GB02's counterexample, not an uncertainty about (26). A nonconvergent bounded
sequence must likewise retain its time-dependent coefficient.

At t_N=N^1/2 the error in (25), after multiplication by N, is only O(1),
and at t_N=N^3/4 it need not be bounded by a vanishing term. The submitted
saturated limit cannot be inferred at either scale. GB03 records that logical
range rejection. It does not claim a numerical counterexample to the actual
source's still separate square-root-time behavior. No growing-time boundary
CMI comparison is established by this microscopic argument.

## 5. Independent finite forcing proof for B03

This proof is finite; no unknown asymptotic constant enters the N5000 interval.
Put γ=1+1/N. Taylor expansion in the self-exclusion displacement gives

    Ls_0=−γs_0+tanh m+R,
    |E[R_t s_0(0)]|≤μ2/N+1/N².                  (27)

Indeed tanh(m−s/N)=tanh m−(s/N)sech²m+η, |η|≤1/N², since
|tanh''|≤2. Splitting −(s/N)sech²m into −s/N+(s/N)tanh²m and using
|tanh m|≤|m| proves (27).

Let g(x)=tanh x−x. Equation (15) and its sign imply
−x⁴/3≤xg(x)≤0. Exchangeability and m_0=m_t+(m_0−m_t) give

    E[g(m_t)m_0]∈[−μ4/3−sqrt(2t μ6/N)/3,
                             sqrt(2t μ6/N)/3].  (28)

The Cauchy payment in (28) uses exactly (13); it is not an assumed slow-mode
approximation. Combining (13), (27), and (28) bounds the instantaneous
forcing c'+γc between

    μ2−μ4/3−A_N(t)−t/N   and   μ2+A_N(t),
    A_N(t)=μ2/N+1/N²+sqrt(2t μ6/N)/3.             (29)

The function A_N is nondecreasing. Integrate against e^−γ(t−u), let
h(t)=(1−e^−γt)/γ, and use
∫_0^t u e^−γ(t−u)du=(t−h(t))/γ. This proves for every finite N,t≥0

    c_lo(t)=e^−γt+μ2h−[A_N(t)+μ4/3]h−(t−h)/(Nγ),
    c_hi(t)=e^−γt+μ2h+A_N(t)h,
    c_lo(t)≤c_N(t)≤c_hi(t).                       (30)

Every sign and endpoint in the new code follows from (29)–(30). The output
separately records self-exclusion, nonlinearity, magnetization loss and all
integration factors at t and 2t.

For positive x intervals x_L≤x≤x_U<1, and z_L≤z≤z_U, define

    D_L=z_L−x_U²,   D_U=z_U−x_L².                (31)

At each submitted finite wait, D_L>0 is checked. Since all four absolute
cell differences in (19) equal D/4, Pinsker in natural logs gives

    I≥||P−Q||_1²/2=D²/2≥D_L²/2.                (32)

The inequality log r≤r−1 gives

    I≤χ²(P,Q)=D²/(1−x²)²≤D_U²/(1−x_U²)².         (33)

The identity in (33) follows by summing 1/Q_uv=16/(1−x²)².
The new calculation checks positive P and Q on its whole independent x,z
rectangle, uses directed Arb throughout, and saves exact rational outward
endpoints. All source rows enter (11)'s finite moment operands; no moment
or probability is replaced by a midpoint. There is no source tail to pay.
Rounding is paid by Arb followed by outward dyadic endpoint rounding.

The complete short-wait interval at t=1/3 is a declared **inherited completed
input**, copied byte-for-byte from the earlier source-bound result. The new
wait-four interval is reconstructed independently. Comparing its lower with
the inherited upper is valid because the source, stationary recipient and
physical-site attempt clock are the same. This track does not claim that
reading that completed interval independently redoes its old KL panel.

An implementation detail caused attempt01's retained failure. The earlier
calculation and this calculation first round their correlation enclosures to
outward binary endpoints. The resulting lower-bound quantities D_L²/2 are
therefore slightly different valid numbers. Their tiny arithmetic uncertainty
balls need not overlap. Attempt02/03 retain the actual signed discrepancies;
the original conservative display and strict rate/information margins remain
the comparisons. No scientific formula or input changed to remove that failure.

## 6. Sparse small-source qualification and all numerical payments

The fresh small calculation directly enumerates all 64 N6 configurations and
computes their energies by summing every physical pair. It verifies (1),
normalization and every detailed-balance edge using rates obtained from (2).
It neither runs earlier analysis code nor assumes a dynamical quotient.

Use T=I+L/N. This is the genuine stochastic attempted-update transition
operator. For any bounded observable f,

    e^{tL}f=e^−Nt Σ_{k≥0}(Nt)^k T^k f/k!.        (34)

The runner applies T through sparse physical neighbors only. It never builds
a dense source matrix or exponential. If λ=Nt and K+2>λ, the remaining
Poisson probability is bounded by

    e^−λ λ^(K+1)/(K+1)! /[1−λ/(K+2)].            (35)

This is the decreasing-ratio geometric majorant. Stochasticity gives
||T^k f||∞≤||f||∞. Hence a correlation whose other observable is bounded
by one has absolute omitted error at most ||f||∞ times (35). The code
pays that symmetric error for f=s_0,m,tanh m, and Ls_0, using bounds one,
one, one and two respectively. Its registered fixed K gives tail below
2^−190; the actual tail, every term count and all directed arithmetic are
retained. The output compares (13), (14), (16), (17), and (30) to this
source-identical finite sparse calculation and checks the four-cell KL bounds.
These finite checks qualify implementation; the proofs above establish the
all-N and all-time claims.

For GB01's nonconstant wrong-Gibbs control, replacing β=1/N by 1/(2N) while
retaining the original rates yields a separately computed N6 second moment
and a nonzero flux imbalance on a declared edge. Both discrepancies are
directed and retained. Merely adding a constant to H would change the
partition normalization but leave every normalized moment unchanged; that
inapplicable expectation is expressly excluded from the registered control.

## 7. Cycle costs and the exact comparison in B04

Let I_4≥L_4>0 and I_short≤U_short, with short wait 1/3. Under independent
stationary preparation/readout cost p≥0, identical in the two experiments,
the cycle times are 8+p and 2/3+p. The information-per-cycle-time ratio obeys

    [I_4/(8+p)]/[I_short/(2/3+p)]
      ≥(L_4/U_short)(2/3+p)/(8+p).               (36)

Differentiating the last factor gives (8−2/3)/(8+p)²>0. Therefore its
minimum on p≥0 is the zero-overhead value, (L_4/U_short)/12. The result
files certify this lower strictly above 1.84, while L_4/U_short>22.08.

This is a ratio of information per declared independently prepared triple's
cycle cost. It does not assign physical seconds, reset or equilibration time,
a capacity of a communication channel, independence to overlapping samples,
or a measurement-noise model.

The equal-overhead hypothesis matters. GB03 gives the long-wait preparation
cost 1000 and the short-wait preparation cost zero, keeping both information
certificates. The new long-wait *upper* rate is below the inherited short-wait
*lower* rate. Thus an explicitly changed cost can reverse the finite comparison;
it cannot inherit the identical-cost conclusion.

## 8. Distinct leading timing objectives and their optima

The microscopic fixed-time information coefficient is proportional to
A(t)=tanh²(t/2). Its information itself increases to its limiting coefficient;
its information per zero-overhead cycle time is proportional to A(t)/(2t).
Since (log A)'=2/sinh t,

    d log[A(t)/(2t)]/dt=2/sinh t−1/t.             (37)

The positive critical point satisfies sinh t=2t. The function
sinh t−2t has derivative cosh t−2, which changes sign once; its second
derivative is positive for t>0. Starting at zero with negative derivative,
it crosses zero exactly once at a positive time. The objective tends to zero
at both 0 and infinity, so that point is its unique global leading-objective
maximum. The directed rational bracket and refined root are retained.

The completed boundary fixed-time coefficient is proportional to
B(t)=t⁴/(e^{2t}−1)². The microscopic derivation does not substitute for this
separate boundary law. Its logarithmic derivative is

    (log B)'=4/t−4/(1−e^−2t).                    (38)

The boundary information itself is maximal at t=1−e^−2t. The boundary
information per cycle time B(t)/(2t) is maximal at
4t=3(1−e^−2t). Each defining right-minus-left function is strictly concave,
starts at zero with positive derivative, and eventually becomes negative;
it has exactly one positive root. Both objectives tend to zero at both
endpoints. Thus these are the separate unique leading maxima, not competing
estimates of the same objective. The runner certifies the submitted rational
sign brackets, bisects with directed signs, and evaluates leading coefficient
and per-time ratios relative to t=1/3.

With fixed p≥0, the microscopic leading objective A(t)/(2t+p) instead has
stationary equation sinh t=2t+p. The same derivative argument gives one
positive maximum (with the nonzero root understood at p=0). The boundary
leading derivative becomes

    4/t−4/(1−e^−2t)−2/(2t+p)=0.                 (39)

These are properties of the displayed leading objectives. If p=p_N grows
polynomially, the microscopic surrogate root grows like log(2p_N); for
p_N=N^a with a>0 this lies in the declared growing range. This does not
control derivatives of the true finite-N remainder or establish a finite-N
optimizer. No global optimum over all times or physical cycle rate is inferred.

For completeness, the earlier timing deduction also concerns the reversible
Markov defect. For any finite reversible stationary source and centered
norm-one observable, c(t)=∫e^−tλ dν(λ). Differentiating its logarithm gives
(log c)''=Var_{ν_t}(λ)≥0, strictly if ν is not a point mass. At fixed total
wait T, Jensen gives c(t)c(T−t)≥c(T/2)². Thus
c(T)−c(t)c(T−t) is maximal at equal waits, uniquely for T>0 and a
non-point spectral measure. A point measure gives zero defect at every split.
The reference-cell probabilities vary with the split, so this does not by
itself optimize CMI. The fresh runner includes a strict two-atom diagnostic.

The one-pair stationary control with |ρ|<1 has
L s_0=−s_0+ρs_1, Ls_1=−s_1+ρs_0 and initial correlation ρ. Solving
those two equations gives

    c_ρ(t)=e^−t[cosh(ρt)+ρsinh(ρt)],
    D_ρ(t,s)=(1−ρ²)e^−(t+s)sinh(ρt)sinh(ρs).     (40)

For equal waits and 0<|ρ|<1, the derivative of log D is
−2+2|ρ|coth(|ρ|t), giving the unique maximum
atanh(|ρ|)/|ρ|, which tends to one as |ρ|→0. At ρ=0 the defect is
identically zero, and at |ρ|=1 it is also zero; neither degeneracy has a
unique defect maximum. Dropping the initial Gibbs term in (40) produces
c'_ρ(0)=−1 instead of −1+ρ². GB01 records the exact discrepancy 1/9 at
ρ=1/3, so the corrected stationary law is retained throughout.

## 9. Exact scope of the result

B01–B03 concern the actual uniform complete positive-fill source at critical
whole scaling, not signed_packet, signed_chain, fixed sparse-strength,
boundary heatbath, accepted-mass memory, or a physically calibrated isotope
law. Stationarity, inversion symmetry, permutation symmetry and the original
site attempt clock are explicit used premises. B02 retains t_0>0, the positive
cell margin and the two separate limits t_N→∞ and t_N=o(sqrtN).
B04 distinguishes three leading objectives, fixed-total defect optimization,
and the finite equal-cost certificate. None asserts a global finite-source
optimum, calibrated seconds, arbitrary kinetics, dependent-sample rates or
uncharged preparation.

Every numerical result belongs to the retained runner outputs. The short-wait
comparison interval and the boundary leading fixed-time source law are inherited
completed inputs; their use is identified explicitly. The new all-time and
uniform estimates are analytic, the counting controls are exact host arithmetic,
and the scalar/sparse/timing finite computations use directed host Arb. No
native-engine, different-model, external-human or director-approval status is
assigned by this track. Next: review the written proof and hypotheses together
with the public replay and all retained failed attempts.

The broad all-time bound does not claim an actual nonzero fixed-N long-time plateau. Static constants are uniform in N and time-independent, with numerical all-N values uncomputed. |

Reset, equilibration, dependence, physical seconds, channel capacity and detector noise are unassigned. Different overhead cannot inherit this comparison. |

Positive/zero-overhead derivative formulas retained. No true finite-N global optimizer or derivative control of the true remainder is inferred. |

| QC03: wrong signs/unpaid tail | HELD | Reversing each quadrature sign excludes the true three-atom value. An unpaid K2 KL sum excludes the rational-law information; adding the explicit series payment restores an enclosure. |

## Signed finite receiver

At stationary physical-site waits 1/3 and 1/3, the independently computed

    0.0000034208196027 <= I <= 0.0000034208514025 nats.

The exact directed rational endpoints are in

# Independent quadrature and information derivation

The assigned statements C01–C03 hold with the source, clock and moment-producer
hypotheses below. The mathematics here is reconstructed directly. Finite checks
qualify the implementation; they are not the arguments for all sources or waits.
The original moment-contraction producer remains an explicitly inherited
operand of the N5000 receiver test. Lead review and a fresh public replay are the
next step.

## 1. The actual source and its spectral measure (C01)

Let the spins be s in {-1,+1}^N. The sealed positive-fill signed_packet parent
has N=5000, zero fields, 1000 disjoint negative pairs and 3000 other spins.
Every pair not negatively overridden has coupling +1. Thus, writing M=sum s_i,

    H(s)=-(M²-N)/2+2 sum_{negative pairs {i,j}} s_i s_j,
    pi(s)=Z^(-1) exp[-H(s)/N].

This is a positive probability on every configuration, with the absolute Z of
this source. The physical generator uses one heatbath attempt at each site:

    Lg(s)=sum_i r_i(s)[g(s^i)-g(s)],
    r_i(s)=1/{1+exp[(H(s^i)-H(s))/N]}.

For a negatively paired site of sign u and partner sign v,

    H(s^i)-H(s)=2u(M-u-2v).

For an unpaired site the expression is 2u(M-u). All N sites are updated;
unpaired here means free of the negative correction, not free of the dense
interaction. Detailed balance follows immediately:

    pi(s) r_i(s)=pi(s^i) r_i(s^i).

Every flip rate is strictly positive, so the hypercube chain is irreducible.
Its stationary law is the displayed pi. In L²(pi), let P_i be conditional
expectation over spin i. Then P_i is an orthogonal projection and

    A=-L=sum_i (I-P_i),
    <g,Ag>_pi=(1/2)sum_{s,i} pi(s)r_i(s)[g(s^i)-g(s)]² >=0.

Hence A is self-adjoint and 0<=A<=N I. The kernel consists only of constants.
Global spin inversion preserves H and every rate. For f(s)=s_0,
<f,1>_pi=0 and <f,f>_pi=1. The finite spectral resolution of A therefore gives
the probability measure

    nu=sum_lambda ||E_lambda f||_pi² delta_lambda,
    supp(nu) subset (0,N],
    mu_j=<f,A^j f>_pi=int lambda^j dnu,
    c(t)=<f,exp(-tA)f>_pi=int exp(-t lambda)dnu.

The lower anchor zero is valid even though the actual centered irreducible
source has no mass there. A uniform gap in N is not being assumed. The proof
below also applies to any positive measure on [0,infinity) with the required
finite moments. It needs no chosen upper cutoff. For site attempt rates alpha_i,
the same argument has A=sum alpha_i(I-P_i) and upper bound sum alpha_i; changing
the clock without changing times changes the correlation being certified.

The replay reads the actual parent, checks its ports [0,1,60,61], every negative
edge, disjointness and zero fields, and checks the actual root15 restriction.
The compressed parent byte hash 44b7476b... and its stored scientific source tag
12b7e5a0... name different objects. Both are retained, without identifying the
scientific tag with the compressed-file SHA-256.

## 2. Hankel geometry and Gauss exactness (C01)

For m>=1 and polynomial p(lambda)=sum_{j=0}^{m-1} p_j lambda^j, put

    G_ij=mu_(i+j), D_ij=mu_(i+j+1), i,j=0,...,m-1.

Then p^T Gp=int p² dnu and p^T Dp=int lambda p² dnu. In particular G and D
are positive semidefinite, and D<=R G when supp(nu) subset [0,R]. A raw list
with G>=0 is not thereby a compatible nonnegative-support moment list: shifted
positivity, singular extension consistency and source existence matter too.

Suppose G>0. Compression of multiplication by lambda onto polynomials of degree
<m has matrix B=G^(-1)D. It is self-adjoint in the G inner product because
GB=D=D^T. Its eigenvalues lie in the support's closed convex hull by Rayleigh
quotients. The identities B e_j=e_(j+1), j<m-1, make e_0 cyclic. A self-adjoint
matrix with a cyclic vector has simple eigenvalues and nonzero projection of
that vector onto every eigenvector: otherwise an eigenspace contains a vector
orthogonal to every B^j e_0. Thus its m nodes xi_i are distinct and its weights
w_i=||E_i e_0||_G² are strictly positive, with sum_i w_i=mu_0=1.

For completeness, the monic orthogonal polynomial P_m is obtained by solving
G times its lower coefficient vector = -(mu_m,...,mu_(2m-1))^T. The compression
identity gives P_m(B)e_0=0, so its roots are the nodes. Divide any polynomial
p of degree <=2m-1 as p=q P_m+r, deg(q),deg(r)<m. Orthogonality gives
int qP_m dnu=0; at every node qP_m vanishes. For r, B^j e_0=e_j yields

    int r dnu=e_0^T G r(B)e_0=sum_i w_i r(xi_i).

Consequently the rule integrates every polynomial through degree 2m-1 exactly.
The argument includes an endpoint node under exact support closure. For a
positive measure with more than m support points, the nodes are interior to
the support hull. The finite-source nodes are nonnegative in every case.

Let F be exp(-t lambda), t>=0. Its Hermite interpolant H of degree <=2m-1
matches F and F' at all nodes. The repeated-node Rolle remainder, on the finite
interval containing a particular lambda and all nodes, is

    F(lambda)-H(lambda)
      =F^(2m)(zeta)/(2m)! * product_i (lambda-xi_i)² >=0.

At a node the difference is zero. This pointwise argument also works on an
unbounded support: no common finite interpolation interval is required, and
the interpolating polynomial has the assumed integrable moments. Exactness
of H then gives

    L_m(t)=sum_i w_i exp(-t xi_i)
          =e_0^T G exp(-tB)e_0 <=c(t).

At t=0 both sides equal one. At t>0 the derivative in the remainder is strictly
positive; equality holds precisely when nu is supported on the quadrature
nodes. This is a lower bound, not an upper bound. QC03 reverses it on an
explicit three-atom measure and obtains a strict contradiction.

## 3. The shifted measure and left-zero Radau upper (C01)

Use the nonnormalized positive measure eta(dlambda)=lambda nu(dlambda):

    G+_ij=mu_(i+j+1), D+_ij=mu_(i+j+2), B+=(G+)^(-1)D+.

Its total mass is mu_1, not one. The first-row contraction with G+ retains
this normalization. If G+>0, D+>0 also: int lambda² p² dnu=0 would force p to
vanish eta-almost everywhere and contradict int p² deta>0. Therefore B+ has
strictly positive nodes, even if nu has a zero atom.

Define the entire function

    h_t(lambda)=int_0^t exp(-u lambda)du,
    h_t(0)=t.

All its even lambda derivatives are nonnegative, being integrals of
u^(2m)exp(-u lambda). Applying the Gauss argument to eta and h_t gives

    int h_t deta >= e_0^T G+ h_t(B+)e_0,
    c(t)=1-int h_t deta <= U_m(t),
    U_m(t)=1-e_0^T G+ h_t(B+)e_0.

For invertible B+ this is
1-e_0^T G+ (B+)^(-1)[I-exp(-tB+)]e_0, with exactly the displayed minus sign.
The replay uses the upper-right column of

    exp [[-tB+, t e_0], [0,0]],

which equals h_t(B+)e_0. This computes the analytic function without dividing
by a nearly zero eigenvalue. It does not authorize inversion of a singular G+.

To identify the Radau rule, let a_i>0 be the shifted Gauss weights at eta_i>0.
Put W_i=a_i/eta_i and W_0=1-sum_i W_i. For a polynomial p of degree <=2m,
write p(lambda)=p(0)+lambda r(lambda). Shifted Gauss exactness gives

    int p dnu=W_0 p(0)+sum_i W_i p(eta_i).

All W_i>0. The polynomial q(lambda)=product_i(1-lambda/eta_i)² is nonnegative,
has degree 2m, q(0)=1 and vanishes at eta_i. Thus W_0=int q dnu>=0. This proves
normalization and positivity, including a possible zero endpoint weight.
Equivalently,

    U_m(t)=W_0+sum_i W_i exp(-t eta_i).

The rule is a left-endpoint-zero Radau upper. At t=0 it is one. At positive t
it equals c exactly when the positive part of nu is supported at the shifted
nodes; nu may additionally have an atom at zero. In particular a one-positive-
atom measure has Gauss and Radau equality, and a zero-plus-m-positive-atom
measure has exact m-node shifted Radau. These are explicit replay controls.

Because L_m(t)>=0 and U_m(t)>=c(t)>=0, for t,s>=0

    L_m(t+s)-U_m(t)U_m(s) <= Delta(t,s)
      <=U_m(t+s)-L_m(t)L_m(s),
    Delta=c(t+s)-c(t)c(s).

Both inequalities retain the correct product directions. An inconclusive
lower bound is kept; it is not a reason to assert that actual Delta is zero.

## 4. Singular cases and what interval arithmetic may certify (C01, QC01–02)

Suppose the moments are known to come from a positive measure. A nonzero
kernel polynomial p of G_m satisfies int p² dnu=0. It therefore vanishes on
the entire support, so the support is finite, among the nonnegative real roots
of p. Quotienting polynomial space by the zero-norm ideal gives an exact
finite multiplication operator. The corresponding exponential is the exact
correlation, at every t. No limit of an unstable inverse is needed. If G+
is singular, int lambda p² dnu=0 puts the positive support among the roots of
p; any remaining mass is at zero and is fixed by total mass one.

For a proposed exact raw sequence, singularity additionally imposes all
available recurrence relations. If p(lambda)=sum_{j=0}^r p_j lambda^j has zero
norm, then sum_j p_j mu_(k+j)=0 at every available k. A proposed extension
violating one of these equations is incompatible. Roots must be real and
nonnegative, and recovered weights must be nonnegative and normalized. The
replay constructs the monic annihilator exactly, checks every available
recurrence, isolates its exact real roots with SymPy and solves its exact
Vandermonde weights. Nonsingular truncated necessary tests are explicitly
reported only as necessary tests; they do not establish source existence.

If mu_1=0, nonnegative support forces nu=delta_0 and c(t)=1. A single positive
atom nu=delta_a gives c(t)=exp(-at), Delta=0 and exact one-node rules; asking
for additional nodes produces singular Grams and should invoke this closure.
With a genuine source measure, G>0 and G+>0 suffice for the stated receiver.
Negative pivots invalidate these hypotheses. An interval pivot containing
zero establishes neither positivity nor exact closure: the replay returns
INSUFFICIENT_OR_NONPOSITIVE, never silently drops a coordinate.

QC01 includes mu=(1,1,0), a negative-support point sequence, a point outside a
declared finite cutoff, the incompatible extension (1,1,1,2), and a deliberately
uncertain pivot. The first execution rejected the incompatible extension via
an upper-support localizer before reaching the harness's expected recurrence
subtype. Its assertion failure is preserved. The successor tests recurrence
without that optional upper cutoff; the rejection condition is unchanged.

QC02 includes delta_0, delta_2, (delta_0+2delta_2)/3 and
nu=delta_0/4+delta_1/2+delta_3/4. It checks exact finite closure, one-atom
Gauss/Radau equality, left-zero endpoint equality, positive normalized weights
and interior Gauss nodes. For the last measure the two shifted nodes are its
two positive atoms, so Radau is exact while two-node Gauss is strictly lower.

## 5. Exact binary law and defect (C02)

For binary readings (A,B,C), Fourier inversion on {-1,+1}³ expresses their law
in the eight characters 1,A,B,C,AB,BC,AC,ABC. Global simultaneous sign reversal
annuls exactly the odd characters. Consequently, with x=E AB, y=E BC, z=E AC,

    p(a,b,c)=[1+xab+ybc+zac]/8.

For stationary readings of the same spin at 0,t,t+s, these moments are
c(t),c(s),c(t+s). Inversion is supplied by this source and dynamics, not by
stationarity alone. A stationary i.i.d. spin with P(+)=3/4 has mean 1/2 and
triple moment 1/8, and is an exact control against deleting odd coefficients.

The middle marginal is 1/2. The adjacent-marginal-matched Markov reference is

    q(a,b,c)=p_AB(a,b)p_BC(b,c)/p_B(b)
            =(1+xab)(1+ybc)/8.

Set u=ab, v=bc. Each increment pair has two equally probable middle signs, so
the full conditional mutual information is exactly

    I(A;C|B)=D(P||Q),
    P_uv=(1+xu+yv+zuv)/4,
    Q_uv=(1+xu)(1+yv)/4,
    P_uv-Q_uv=Delta uv/4, Delta=z-xy.

This reduction is stationary CMI. It is not the KL of a conditioned accepted
history against an independently chosen reset reference. The replay separately
constructs all eight rational cells and their actual adjacent marginals, then
checks the four-cell identity and eight-cell KL.

For this reversible source,

    Delta(t,s)=Cov_nu(exp(-t Lambda),exp(-s Lambda))
     =(1/2)E[(exp(-tLambda)-exp(-tLambda'))
              (exp(-sLambda)-exp(-sLambda'))]>=0.

Both functions are strictly decreasing for positive waits. Hence Delta=0 at
positive t,s exactly for a one-atom measure. Using their Lipschitz constants
t and s gives Delta<=ts Var_nu(Lambda). The inequality is strict for a
non-point finite measure at positive waits. In particular actual nonzero
spectral dispersion is not a fitting artifact.

## 6. Stable information, reference cells and full tail payment (C02, QC03)

If |x|,|y|<1, all Q_uv>0. Algebra gives the exact chi-square expression

    chi²(P,Q)=sum (P-Q)²/Q
             =Delta²/[(1-x²)(1-y²)].

From log r<=r-1, I<=chi². A useful lower is Pinsker I>=Delta²/2, since the
four absolute cell differences sum to |Delta|. One can obtain this bound by
grouping the two positive-difference cells and applying the binary KL bound:
its second derivative is >=4, so a probability displacement d costs >=2d².

Let d_uv=Delta uv/4. For F(v)=sum (Q+v d)log[(Q+v d)/Q], F(0)=F'(0)=0 and
F''(v)=sum d²/(Q+v d). Therefore, when the segment cells are positive,

    I=(Delta²/16)int_0^1 (1-v)sum_uv 1/(Q_uv+v Delta uv/4)dv.

If theta=max_uv |Delta|/(4Q_uv)<1, then a local Taylor expansion yields

    |I-Delta²/[2(1-x²)(1-y²)]|
      <= |Delta|³/[384(1-theta)²] sum_uv Q_uv^(-2).

Indeed the third derivative in Delta of a cell contribution has absolute
value <=1/[64(1-theta)² Q²]; multiplying its uniform bound by |Delta|³/6
gives this constant. Uniformity over sources or waits requires an actual
uniform lower reference-cell margin and theta bounded away from one. Positive
waits by themselves do not supply that source-uniform margin.

The replay avoids a cancellation of first-order terms. Put r_uv=d_uv/Q_uv.
Since sum d_uv=0,

    I=sum_uv Q_uv [(1+r_uv)log(1+r_uv)-r_uv],
    (1+r)log(1+r)-r=sum_{k=2}^infinity (-1)^k r^k/[k(k-1)].

For rho>=|r|, rho<1, truncation after k=K has absolute cell tail at most

    Q rho^(K+1)/[K(K+1)(1-rho)].

This follows by replacing all later denominators by K(K+1) and summing the
geometric series. Every directed evaluation uses an outward upper bound of
Q and rho and adds the sum of these payments as a symmetric error ball.
Arb also pays all arithmetic rounding. Correlation boxes, Delta, Q and every
polynomial operation retain interval dependence conservatively. Direct KL
overlaps the paid series as an independent formula check. The signed receiver
uses K=32; the rarer-cell diagnostic uses K=128.

The wrong control x=y=0,z=1/2 has exact information

    (3/4)log(3/2)+(1/4)log(1/2).

The unpaid K=2 sum is exactly 1/8 and is strictly smaller. Its rounding-only
interval excludes the exact value. The explicit series tail restores an
enclosure. Thus the control detects omission of the tail, without claiming
that the exact nonnegative information itself failed.

If an adjacent pair is deterministic, |x|=1 or |y|=1, the valid triple has
I=0 directly because one endpoint is fixed by the middle. A zero-denominator
chi-square formula is not used there. Standard zero-cell conventions apply
to the exact KL identity; the replay's nondegenerate cases verify strict
positivity before taking logarithms.

## 7. The rare-cell limit and heatbath clock identity (C02)

For a fixed finite source, c is analytic. Suppose

    c(u)=1-a u+b u²/2+O(u³), a>0, b>=a²>0.

The spectral representation supplies b>=a². Factor the vanishing cells before
forming logarithms. In particular,

    P_--=[1-c(t)-c(s)+c(t+s)]/4
        =(1/4)int_0^t int_0^s c''(u+v)dvdu
        =bts/4+O(ts(t+s)),
    Q_--=a²ts/4+O(ts(t+s)).

The one-minus cells have respective factors s and t and leading positive
coefficients a/2. The ++ cell has leading one. After these common factors
are canceled in each logarithmic ratio, all factored functions are analytic
and strictly positive on a fixed source-dependent neighborhood of (0,0).
The mixed defect is (b-a²)ts+O(ts(t+s)). The ++ contribution has leading
+(b-a²)ts/4; the two one-minus contributions each have leading
-(b-a²)ts/4. Their higher terms are O(ts(t+s)): for example the cell of size
O(s) has relative perturbation O(t), so its quadratic remainder is O(st²).
The -- contribution has leading (bts/4)log(b/a²). Adding gives

    I(t,s)=ts [b log(b/a²)-b+a²]/4+O(ts(t+s)).

This error is uniform in the relative ratio of positive t and s in that
source-dependent neighborhood. Its constant need not be uniform as a tends
to zero or the source changes. It is not obtained by applying a fixed-cell
quadratic formula beyond that formula's margin hypothesis.

For a physical observed-site heatbath attempt alpha, only that site's update
acts on f=s_0. Thus Af=alpha(I-P_0)f and orthogonal projection gives

    b=||Af||²=alpha²||(I-P_0)f||²
     =alpha<f,Af>=alpha a.

Hidden attempt speeds may change higher moments, but not this identity.
For alpha=1 the short-time coefficient is

    C(a)=a[-log a-1+a]/4.

The finite pair control uses rho=1/2, a=b=3/4. Its correct coefficient differs
strictly from the inappropriately uniform quadratic coefficient
(b-a²)²/(8a²). The exact stationary pair correlation is

    c(u)=exp(-u)[cosh(rho u)+rho sinh(rho u)],

which retains the initial Gibbs cross term. The code's diminishing-wait panel
is diagnostic confirmation; the factorization above establishes the law.
The ratio b/a identifies alpha only under this declared heatbath model and
its time unit. It does not assign physical seconds.

## 8. What follows from the supplied signed moment data (C02)

The inherited file ACTUAL_SIGNED_N5000_MOMENTS.json supplies rigorous boxes
through mu_4 from its source-specific matching-pair contraction and paid
48-term insertion tail. That producer is not executed here and is not replaced
by a uniform source. The stored compressed parent hash agrees with the
actual parent read by this track. Its first entries are approximately

    (1, .9840214127956811, .9840214127956811,
        .9842121116395276, .9844059501072606).

The exact structural identity mu_0=1 is imposed. Source existence, reversibility
and the supplied moment enclosures make the preceding receiver applicable.
Directed LDL pivots certify G_2>0, G+_2>0 and D+_2>0; the optional actual
source cutoff tests also pass. Solves and order-two exponentials with the full
moment boxes then enclose c(1/3) and c(2/3), with no midpoint substitution.
Their correlation enclosure produces a strictly positive defect lower. The
stable four-cell evaluation gives outward display

    I in [0.0000034208196027, 0.0000034208514025] nats.

The rational endpoints in the result file are authoritative. This interval
is contained in the submitted [0.0000034208196,0.0000034208515] display.
One-node Gauss/Radau gives a negative defect lower, about -0.0010131, while
the two-node lower is about 0.0012580662. The one-node noncertification is
retained. Finite interval agreement does not independently regenerate or
establish the source-moment producer; that is the SIGNED track's assigned
upstream work. This track establishes the conditional downstream implication
and verifies full-source small controls separately.

## 9. Full-source qualifications and static versus dynamic reduction (C03)

For the full 64-state uniform N6 source at beta=1/6, the replay constructs every
energy and every physical flip rate directly, checks every energy increment
and detailed-balance edge, and obtains mu_0,...,mu_8 from sparse generator
actions in the Gibbs metric. All m=1,...,4 correlation rules bracket the
full-source correlation at both waits. No full generator matrix is formed.

The reference propagation uses P=I+L/N, a nonnegative stochastic operator:

    exp(tL)=exp(-Nt)sum_{k=0}^infinity (Nt)^k P^k/k!.

For |f|<=1, |<f,P^k f>_pi|<=1. After K=96, the absolute omitted correlation
is bounded by the next Poisson term divided by 1-Nt/(K+2), since subsequent
positive terms have no larger ratio. The code adds this tail and all Arb
rounding. This is an independently directed full-state reference, not a dense
matrix exponential or an eigenvalue approximation.

The actual isolated root15 is a declared finite source at beta=1/15. The
replay checks all selected original vertices, each of its 105 effective
couplings against the N5000 parent, and local ordered ports [0,1,3,4]. It
enumerates all 32768 states, all physical flips and the degree-four moments.
All five intervals overlap the supplied corrected root15 intervals. This
finite qualification is not a replacement of the N5000 Gibbs law.

For its three negative pairs and nine other sites, the exact integer
configuration counts by plus count k and number h of opposed negative pairs
are coefficients of

    (1+2 y x+x²)^3 (1+x)^9.

Every coefficient agrees with full enumeration, and their total is 2^15.
At fixed (k,h), H=-[(2k-15)²-15]/2+6-4h. This is an exact static contraction.
Nevertheless take states with plus spins {0,1,2,3,4,5,6,7} and
{0,2,3,4,5,6,7,8}. Both have s_0=+1 and M=1, but partner s_1 is respectively
+1 and -1. Their visible flip rates are respectively

    1/(1+exp(-4/15)), 1/(1+exp(4/15)).

Their difference is strictly positive. Thus the observed-spin/total-count
partition is not strongly lumpable, despite the exact static polynomial.
All count-weighted static inserts may be useful without defining a dynamical
quotient. The same issue remains for the actual signed parent and cannot be
removed by borrowing the uniform source's quotient.

## 10. Fitted clocks and the retained scope boundary (C03)

Fix one declared irreducible reversible boundary generator B with stationary
law pi_b and centered unit observation f. Its spectral correlation

    c_b(u)=int_(0,infinity) exp(-u lambda)drho(lambda)

decreases strictly from one to zero. Therefore, for a given microscopic
adjacent correlation x in (0,1), the equation c_b(kappa t)=x, kappa>0, has
exactly one solution. If x is enclosed rather than known, all possible fits
are enclosed by monotone endpoint brackets. An actual interval sign at each
bracket endpoint is needed for a numerical bracket; a floating root alone
is not such a certificate. This argument covers rescaling that fixed
generator, not a refit of its individual rates or a change of stationary law.

It also explains why an adjacent fit does not identify the entire microscopic
semigroup. The replay gives an exact finite counterexample, explicitly a
changed control source: four independent visible-hidden spin pairs with
stationary pair law (1+rho s_i h_i)/4, rho=1/2. The complete four-port marginal
is exactly uniform. Give each visible site unit attempts and all hidden sites
attempt speed gamma, either 1 or 3. All visible projected edges have the same
rate (1-rho²)/2=3/8 for both gamma values. Full rational enumeration of all 256
states confirms stationarity, all projected edges and moments. Yet

    mu_1=mu_2=3/4,
    mu_3=(3/4)(1+gamma/4),

so their microscopic semigroups differ. The invariant two-function space
span(s_0,h_0) permits an exact order-two compression and directed correlation
evaluation. Both microscopic triples have positive information. The uniform
four-port heatbath observation has c_b(u)=exp(-u) and zero CMI at every
positive scalar rescaling. It fits the adjacent correlation using
kappa=-log c_micro(t)/t, but it cannot fit the positive microscopic defect.

This demonstrates precisely the fitted-clock qualification. For the submitted
signed N5000 result, the actual same-source boundary weights, numerical fit
bracket and reported boundary-information ratio remain upstream SIGNED/A04
operands; this track does not claim to have regenerated those numbers. Its
general receiver and qualification do not expand them to arbitrary kinetics.

The original positive-fill signed-chain factor/tail route is retained as an
unexecuted source adapter. Its component post-flip actions and mixed factorial
inserts are not supplied by the signed_packet moments. No signed-chain CMI,
negative-fill N5000 certificate, all-N signed amplitude, physical seconds,
or accepted-mass trajectory law is inferred here. No such calculation was
started. The next step remains review of these proofs and a fresh public replay.

# CAR/Fock/loading verification — claims held; control witness corrected

All twelve assigned mathematical claims held. Ten controls behaved as
registered; one initial Li6 control selected a true null and did not break.
That failed attempt and the corrected nonzero witness remain in full. Fresh
complete-case relocation reproduced all 24 declared deterministic replay files,
including their metadata/manifests. Final scope is I1/R2. Next is case custody
and incorporation of these source-explicit statements when a revision is chosen.

- [Every verifier report, proof and hypothesis map](FULL_REPORTS_AND_PROOFS.md).
- [All original outcome fields and every claim/control](RESULTS/ALL_TRACK_OUTCOMES.json).
- [Lead proof/code review](RESULTS/LEAD_REVIEW.json), [fresh public replay](RESULTS/LEAD_PUBLIC_REPLAY.json) and [numerical receipt](RESULTS/NUMERICAL_DISPLAY_RECEIPT.json).

The unchanged Li6 source retains its complete raw600 metric, physical phase9,
address14336, every flavor factor, coherent field self/ordered-vertex term,
all internal/cell images and the same complete proper reference. A common
positive-polar one-hop map gives benchmark margin >1.6027627093 and a
same-static-G real own-cloud family margin >1.3129421370. The common source
map and its transported Writes are wider statements than the unchanged-input
energy upper. An actual raw600 pure411 control prevents a uniform-input energy
claim. The sector321 sufficient upper does not promote an all-sector spectral
